9.24994pt\Theta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}}{\Theta_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Theta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\otimes}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-val-Prod}{{{Ty-val-Prod}}}}{\text{Prod}}},}\mpr@andcr\frac{\Theta\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}\hskip-1.1625pt\cdot\hskip-1.1625pt\Theta\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{Mod}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{!}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-val-Exp}{{{Ty-val-Exp}}}}{\text{Exp}}},}\mpr@andcr\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.58122pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\uparrow}}\hskip-1.1625pt\cdot\hskip-1.1625pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},~\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},~{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to^{\scriptscriptstyle\text{-}1}}}\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},~\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,_{{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\mathsf{\pdfliteral hnames\pdfliteral}(}}\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{)}}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathrel{\hskip-0.79999pt\raisebox{-5.03749pt}{\textasciicircum}}\hskip-2.0pt}}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\rangle}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\ltimes}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-val-Ampar}{{{Ty-val-Ampar}}}}{\text{Ampar}}},}

Figure 7.
Runtime values and new typing context forms

The syntax of runtime values is given in
Figure

7
. It features constructors for all of our basic types, as well as functions (note that in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\,\!{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}\!\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}}\,_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}
is a term, not a value). The more interesting values are holes
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}
, destinations
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}
, and ampars
{}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathrel{\hskip-0.88887pt\raisebox{-5.59721pt}{\textasciicircum}}\hskip-2.22221pt}}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\rangle}}
, which we’ll describe in the rest of the section. In order for the operational semantics to use substitution, which requires substituting variables with values, we also extend the syntax of terms to include values through rule
Ty-term-Val
.

Destinations and holes are two faces of the same coin, as seen in
Section

2.1
, and we must ensure that throughout the reduction, a destination always points to a hole, and a hole is always the target of exactly one destination. Thus, the new idea of our system is to feature
hole bindings

{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
and
destination bindings

{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}}
in typing contexts in addition to the usual variable bindings
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
. In both cases, we call
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}
a
hole name
. By definition, a context
\Theta
can contain both destination bindings and hole bindings, but
not a destination binding and a hole binding for the same hole name
.

We extend our previous context operations
+
and
\cdot
to act on the new binding forms, as described in
Figure

7
. Context addition is still very partial; for instance,
({\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}})+({\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n^{\prime}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}})
is not defined, as
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}
is present on both sides but with different binding forms.

One of the main goals of
\lambda_{d}
is to ensure that a hole value is never read. The type system maintains this invariant by simply not allowing any hole bindings in the context when typing terms (see
Figure

7
for the different type of contexts used in the typing judgment). In fact, the only place where holes are introduced, is the left-hand side
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
in an ampar
{}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathrel{\hskip-0.88887pt\raisebox{-5.59721pt}{\textasciicircum}}\hskip-2.22221pt}}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\rangle}}
, in
Ty-val-Ampar
.

Specifically, holes come from the operator
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to^{\scriptscriptstyle\text{-}1}}}
, which represents the matching hole bindings for a set of destination bindings. It’s a partial, pointwise operation on typing contexts
\Delta
, as defined in
Figure

7
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to^{\scriptscriptstyle\text{-}1}}}\Delta
is undefined if any destination binding in
\Delta
has a mode other than
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.6458pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\nu}}
.

Furthermore, in
Ty-val-Ampar
, the holes
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to^{\scriptscriptstyle\text{-}1}}}\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}
and the corresponding destinations
\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}
are bound together and consequently removed from the ampar’s typing context: this is how we ensure that, indeed, there’s one destination per hole and one hole per destination. That being said, both sides of the ampar may also contain stored destinations from other scopes, represented by
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.6458pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\uparrow}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}
in the respective typing contexts of
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
.

Rule
Ty-val-Hole
indicates that a hole must have mode
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.6458pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\nu}}
in typing context to be well-typed; in particular mode coercion is not allowed here, and neither is weakening. Only when a hole is behind an exponential, that mode can change to some arbitrary mode
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}
. The mode of a hole constrains which values can be written to it, e.g. in
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}\!\!\boldsymbol{\phantom{a}{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{v}}\!\!\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{Mod}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{!}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
, only a value with mode
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}
(more precisely, a value typed in a context of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt\Theta
) can be written to
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}
.

Surprisingly, in
Ty-val-Dest
, we see that a destination can be typed with any mode
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}
coercible to
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.6458pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\nu}}
. We did this to mimic the rule
Ty-term-Var
and make the general modal substitution lemma expressible for
\lambda_{d}
5
5

5

Generally, in modal systems, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}},~\Gamma\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}
and
\Delta\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
then
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt\Delta,~\Gamma\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}\mathrel{\!\raisebox{0.38748pt}{$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.43057pt}\resizebox{436906}{0}{=}\hskip-0.86108pt}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v}}]\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}

(
bernardy_modality_2020
)
.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\omega}}\hskip-0.45206pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\infty}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{()}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.45206pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\nu}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}}
so
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\omega}}\hskip-0.6458pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\infty}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt({\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{1}}\hskip-0.45206pt{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\nu}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}})\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{()}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}\mathrel{\!\raisebox{0.38748pt}{$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.43057pt}\resizebox{436906}{0}{=}\hskip-0.86108pt}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\to}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}]\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{1}}
should be valid.
. We formally proved however that throughout a well-typed closed program,
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}
will never be of multiplicity
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\omega}}
or age
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{\infty}}
— a destination is always linear and of finite age — so mode coercion is never actually used; and we used this result during the formal proof of the substitution lemma to make it quite easier. The other mode
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}
, appearing in
Ty-val-Dest
, is not the mode of the destination binding; instead it is part of the type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\lfloor}}\,\!_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rfloor}}
and corresponds to the mode of values that we can write to the corresponding
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\boxed{\hskip 1.29167pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip 1.29167pt}}}
; so for it no coercion can take place.

Other Salient Points

We don’t distinguish values with holes from fully-defined values at the syntactic level: instead types prevent holes from being read. In particular, while values are typed in contexts
\Theta
allowing both destination and hole bindings, when using a value as a term in
Ty-term-Val
, it’s only allowed to have free destinations, but no free holes.

Notice, also, that values can’t have free variables, since contexts
\Theta
only contain hole and destination bindings, no variable binding. That values are closed is a standard feature of denotational semantics or abstract machine semantics. This is true even for function values (
Ty-val-Fun
), which, also is prevented from containing free holes. Since a function’s body is unevaluated, it’s unclear what it’d mean for a function to contain holes; at the very least it’d complicate our system a lot, and we are unaware of any benefit supporting free holes in functions could bring.

One might wonder how we can represent a curried function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}}\,_{\,\,}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}}}\,_{\,\,}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral concat\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}}
at the value level, as the inner abstraction captures the free variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}
. The answer is that such a function, at value level, is encoded as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\,\!{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}\!\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}}\,_{\,\,}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral from\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral\ltimes\pdfliteral}}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral upd\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral\ltimes\pdfliteral}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral new\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral\ltimes\pdfliteral}}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral with\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{d}}\,\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{d}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}}}\,_{\,\,}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral concat\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}}))
, where the inner closure is not yet in value form. As the form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{d}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}}}\,_{\,\,}\!\!\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral concat\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{y}})
is part of term syntax, it’s allowed to have free variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x}}
.

6.2.
Evaluation Contexts and Commands

The semantics of
\lambda_{d}
is given using small-step reductions on a pair
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}{\scriptstyle\bigl[}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}{\scriptstyle\bigr]}
of an evaluation context
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
, and an (extended) term
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}
under focus. We call such a pair
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}{\scriptstyle\bigl[}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}{\scriptstyle\bigr]}
a
command
, borrowing the terminology from
herbelin_curien_2000
.

The grammar of evaluation contexts is given in
Figure

8
. An evaluation context
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
is the composition of an arbitrary number of focusing components
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{e}}
. We chose to represent evaluation contexts syntactically, taking inspiration from
felleisen_calculi_1987
and subsequent
(
danvy_refocusing_2004
;
biernacka_syntactic_2007
)
. The intuition here is that destination filling only require a very tame notion of state. So tame, in fact, that we can simply represent writing to a hole by a substitution in the evaluation context, instead of using more heavy store semantics. With this choice, focusing and defocusing steps are made explicit in the semantics, resulting in a verbose but simpler proof. It is also easier to derive an abstract machine for the language, should one want to do that.

Consequently,
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}{\scriptstyle\bigl[}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}{\scriptstyle\bigr]}
is formally a pair (although we use the notation usually reserved for one-hole contexts, to make rules look more familiar). It’s important to keep in mind that won’t always have a corresponding term (for instance, when
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
contains open ampar focusing components).

Grammar of evaluation contexts:

\!\begin{array}[]{rrl}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{e}}&\mathrel{\raisebox{0.34872pt}{$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.38751pt$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.38751pt}\resizebox{404136}{0}{=}}&{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t^{\prime}}}~\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}\hskip 6.97498pt|\hskip 6.97498pt\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v}}\hskip 6.97498pt|\hskip 6.97498pt\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}\,\fatsemi\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\\

(

hnames\pdfliteral}(}}\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{)}}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathrel{\hskip-0.79999pt\raisebox{-5.03749pt}{\textasciicircum}}\hskip-2.0pt}}~\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\rangle}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-ectxs-OpenAmpar}{{{Ty-ectxs-OpenAmpar}}}}{\text{OpenAmpar}}},}

Figure 8.
Evaluation contexts and their typing rules

Focusing components are all directly derived from the term syntax, except for the
open ampar
component
{}^{\text{op}\!}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathrel{\hskip-0.88887pt\raisebox{-5.59721pt}{\textasciicircum}}\hskip-2.22221pt}}~\raisebox{0.74997pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\rangle}}
. This focusing component indicates that an ampar is currently being processed by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral upd\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral\ltimes\pdfliteral}}}
, with its left-hand side
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
(the structure being built) being attached to the open ampar focusing component, while its right-hand side (containing destinations) is either in subsequent focusing components, or in the term under focus. Ampars being open during the evaluation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral upd\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral\ltimes\pdfliteral}}}
’s body and closed back afterwards is counterpart to the notion of scopes in typing rules.

Evaluation contexts are typed in a context
\Delta
that can only contain destination bindings. As we will later see in rule
Ty-cmd
of
Section

6.3
,
\Delta
is exactly the typing context that the term
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}
has to use to form a valid
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}{\scriptstyle\bigl[}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}{\scriptstyle\bigr]}
. In other words, while
\Gamma\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}

requires
the bindings of
\Gamma
, judgment
\Delta\,\boldsymbol{\dashv}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}

provides
the bindings of
\Delta
. Typing rules for evaluation contexts are given in
Figure

8
.

An evaluation context has a context type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}
. The meaning of
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}
is that given
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}{\scriptstyle\bigl[}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}{\scriptstyle\bigr]}
returns a value of type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}
. Composing an evaluation context
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}
with a new focusing component never affects the type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}
of the future command; only the type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}
of the focus is altered.

All typing rules for evaluation contexts can be derived systematically from the ones for the corresponding term (except for the rule
Ty-ectxs-OpenAmpar
that is a truly new form). Let’s take the rule
Ty-ectxs-PatP
as an example:

\frac{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\otimes}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}\hskip-0.81375pt\cdot\hskip-0.81375pt\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-term-PatP}{{{Ty-term-PatP}}}}{\text{PatP}}},}

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}\hskip-0.81375pt\cdot\hskip-0.81375pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},~\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\,\boldsymbol{\dashv}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U}}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\boldsymbol{\dashv}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\hskip 3.21992pt\circ\hskip 3.21992pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}~\raisebox{0.57747pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\otimes}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{)}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-ectxs-PatP}{{{Ty-ectxs-PatP}}}}{\text{PatP}}},}

•

the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},~\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}
in the premise for
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
corresponds to
{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}\hskip-1.29167pt\cdot\hskip-1.29167pt\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
in the conclusion of
Ty-term-PatP
;

•

the typing context
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
in the premise for term
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}
corresponds to the typing context
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}},~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}:\!_{\!{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
for the same term in
Ty-term-PatP
;

•

the typing context
\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}
in the conclusion for
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\hskip 3.99994pt\circ\hskip 3.99994pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}~\raisebox{0.74997pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}
corresponds to the typing context
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
in the premise for
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}
in
Ty-term-PatP
(the term
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}
is located where the focus
\scriptstyle[]
is in
Ty-ectxs-PatP
).

We think of the typing rule for an evaluation context as a rotation of the typing rule for the associated term, where the typing contexts of one subterm and the conclusion are swapped, and the typing contexts of the other potential subterms are kept unchanged (with the difference that typing contexts for evaluation contexts are of shape
\Delta
instead of
\Gamma
).

6.3.
Small-Step Semantics

We equip
\lambda_{d}
with small-step semantics. There are three sorts of semantic rules:

•

focus rules, where we focus on a subterm of a term, by pushing a corresponding focusing component on the stack
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
;

•

unfocus rules, where the term under focus is in fact a value, and thus we pop a focusing component from the stack
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}
and transform it back to the corresponding term so that a redex appears (or so that another focus/unfocus rule can be triggered);

•

reduction rules, where the actual computation logic takes place.

Here the focus, unfocus, and reduction rules for
PatP
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\,\big]~~\longrightarrow~~{\scriptstyle\bigl(}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\hskip 3.6999pt\circ\hskip 3.6999pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}~\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}{\scriptstyle\bigr)}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\,\big]\hskip 9.24994pt\textit{when}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{NotVal }{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\texttt{ }\\
{\scriptstyle\bigl(}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\hskip 3.6999pt\circ\hskip 3.6999pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}~\raisebox{0.69371pt}{$\scriptstyle[]$}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}{\scriptstyle\bigr)}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v}}\,\big]~~\longrightarrow~~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\,\big]\\
{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral case\pdfliteral}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.7656,0.1797,0.5313}\mathsf{m}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\pdfliteral of\pdfliteral}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{,}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{)}}\boldsymbol{\mapsto}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}\,\big]~~\longrightarrow~~{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}~\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{u}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathrel{\!\raisebox{0.34872pt}{$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.38751pt}\resizebox{404136}{0}{=}\hskip-0.77498pt}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathrel{\!\raisebox{0.34872pt}{$\mathop{:}$\hskip-0.38751pt}\resizebox{404136}{0}{=}\hskip-0.77498pt}{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{v_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}]\,\big]\\
\end{array}

Rules are triggered in a purely deterministic fashion; once a subterm is a value, it cannot be focused on again. Focusing and defocusing rules are entirely mechanical —they are just a matter of pushing or popping a focusing component on the stack— so we only present the set of reduction rules for the system in
Section

6.3
, but the whole system is included in the annex (
and
).

\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\,\big]\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}

(
Typing judgment for commands
)

\frac{\Delta\,\boldsymbol{\dashv}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{\rightarrowtail}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Delta\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{T}}}{\,\boldsymbol{\vdash}\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{E}}\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0.3906,0.3906,0.3906}\mathsf{t}}\,\big]\boldsymbol{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.1875,0.4375}\mathsf{U_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:Ty-cmd}{{{Ty-cmd}}}}{\text{Ty-cmd}}},}

Name set shift and conditional name shift:

\!\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\raisebox{-0.52309pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{${\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+}}{{=}}}}$}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h^{\prime}}}&\boldsymbol{\triangleq}&\{{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip-0.77498pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{+}}\hskip-0.77498pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h^{\prime}}}~|~{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{[}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\raisebox{-0.52309pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{${\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+}}{{=}}}}$}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{]}}&\boldsymbol{\triangleq}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\hskip-0.77498pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{+}}\hskip-0.77498pt{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h^{\prime}}}&\text{if}~{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{h}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.9453,0.293,0.1758}\mathit{H}}\\

Special substitution for open ampars:
